We proceed with the examination of item 17 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the law - Code of Administrative Procedure and some other laws (print No 687). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice, Ms Anna Dalkowska, to present the justification for the bill. [[The statement of the Undersecretary of State is displayed on the screens]]
ParlaMint-PL_2020-11-27-sejm-21-3.u563	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The draft law amending the Act - Code of Administrative Procedure and some other laws, contained in print No. 687, which I have the honour to present on behalf of the Government, contains regulations aimed at enabling the issuing of certificates in electronic form bearing electronic seals. Such a solution will facilitate the procedures for issuing certificates from the National Criminal Register as well as certificates issued by other public administrations. The main issue is, of course, the National Criminal Register. Secondly, the project envisages providing information by means of a network service to authorised entities, inter alia. courts, prosecutors and police, which will directly enable the use of information collected in the National Criminal Register in a synchronised and real-time manner. The aim of the project is to streamline and automate the process of providing information from the National Criminal Register. This objective is to be achieved, firstly, by introducing into the Code of Administrative Procedure the possibility for public administrations, including, as I mentioned, by the National Criminal Register, certificates in electronic form bearing a qualified electronic seal via the electronic system without human interference. Such automated provision of information will enable the applicant to obtain information from the KRK almost immediately after such a request has been made. Secondly, the aim of the project will be achieved by changing the way in which information is provided under the National Criminal Register Act by introducing a solution enabling legitimate entities such as courts, prosecutors, police, special services, obtaining information on demand via the provided network service, web service. The response to the request shall be given in a synchronous manner, i.e. without the participation of a KRK employee, using an electronic seal mechanism. The main reason for the project is the huge demand for information obtained from the National Criminal Register. About 3 million requests per year have been received from KRK for several years. However, the way information is provided has not changed in its assumptions since the creation of the National Criminal Register, i.e. since 2000, when the need for information from the KRK has been many times smaller. The new information solution is intended to provide a greater degree of automation for the processing of this information, inter alia. by providing a network service to specific operators. In order to obtain information from the KRK, these entities will not have to ask questions, but will be able to request such information from the KRK system by means of a specific application they already have at their disposal. On the basis of the request there will be automatic search and verification of the data. The response to the request shall be synchronously provided, i.e. without the participation of a KRK employee. At present, however, it is not possible to fully automate the process of providing such information, because information about a person or a collective entity, regardless of how it is provided, constitutes a certificate within the meaning of the Code of Administrative Procedure, which means that it must be signed in the current legal system by the person authorised by hand or electronically, depending on the form of the certificate chosen. Such a requirement significantly extends the procedure for issuing a certificate, which may adversely affect whether it affects the interests of eligible persons who must receive a certificate for the purpose of, for example, criminal proceedings, tendering, concession proceedings or employment of employees. As regards the amendment of the Code of Administrative Procedure in the form of the possibility of issuing an electronic document accompanied by this special qualified electronic stamp, such a document shall be treated in legal trade on an equal footing with a paper document bearing a handwritten signature or an electronic document bearing a qualified electronic signature. The difference in the proposed solution is how to provide the document with a mechanism to ensure its authenticity. The seal is broadcast not by man, but by the electronics system. Such a solution will also allow for an increase of staffing opportunities, in particular by allocating the staff of the National Criminal Register's Information Office to carry out other tasks. This in turn will result in faster information to other persons and entities. Thank you very much, Minister. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice Anna Dalkowska: Okay, well, maybe not in detail now, but I'm asking on behalf of the government, of course, to support the bill. It is compatible with European Union law. On the other hand, the funds to cover the costs resulting from the proposed law will come from the European Social Fund. Thank you very much, Marshal, High Izbo.

ParlaMint-PL_2020-11-27-sejm-21-3.u565	Thank you. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present our position on the government draft law amending the Act - Code of Administrative Procedure and some other laws contained in Seym Print No. 687. In general, the draft amendment to the Code of Administrative Procedure concerns the issue of certificates. Certificates are needed by different persons and entities in many, many situations. We often meet with the need to present a certificate in matters relating to public procurement, but also for example. a certificate of non-criminality may be required from candidates for different posts for which the law requires no criminal record. Today, the certificate can be obtained in traditional form, i.e. paper form, but also electronically. Surely the electronic form improves the whole procedure, however, art. 217 §4 k.p.a. requires that such electronic certificate be accompanied by a qualified electronic signature. Let me repeat once again: a qualified electronic signature, which means always such a certificate must be signed by a person. Therefore, in the current legal state it is not possible to issue certificates fully automatically by public administrations, because in any case the certificate must be signed by a person authorised to do so. So if we have a paper certificate - it's handheld, and if we have it electronic - it's in the form of an electronic signature. That requirement, as the Minister said... But let us not repeat this again, Member. ... significantly extends the procedure for issuing these certificates. The proposed amendment is intended to improve this situation. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This proposed amendment to the Code of Administrative Procedure also concerns the Act on the National Criminal Register and the Act on the Processing of Criminal Information and is almost identical to the proposal which was made during the previous term of office of the Sejm. Then the Sejm examined the draft at first reading, then the committee adopted the report, but it failed to adopt it. It is therefore necessary to return to this law. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I propose Law and Justice, I request that this project be directed to further work in the Committee on Changes in Codifications. Thank you very much, Member. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2020-11-27-sejm-21-3.u583	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to make a statement on the activist deserved for the municipality of Szubin and for the voivodship of Kujawsko-Pomorskie Andrzej Wichłacz. On 19 November 2020, after a long illness, Andrzej Wwichłacz, a well-deserved social activist, a friend, left. Andrzej Wichłacz was born on 11 August 1949 in Szubina as the son of Franciszek and Stanisława from the house of Boruch. He was a native shubanian and grandson of the insurgent Józef Boruch. Therefore, in his family there were living traditions associated with this break of independence. This tradition is still cultivated by Zofia Drzewieck's granddaughter. Andrzej Wychłacz worked his entire professional life at the State Machinery Centre in Szubina and the Municipal Heat Energy Company in Bydgoszcz. Since 1979, he has been an opposition activist. He has worked in free trade unions since 1980. During the strike in August 1980, he collaborated with the Inter-Bodd Strajkovy Committee in Bydgoszcz and set up the same committee in Szubina with his colleagues. The MKS in Szubina then extended its range to the entire former district of Szubiński. After the registration of the Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union, the MKS became the Inter-Building Founding Committee, the founders of which were the two largest establishments in the municipality of Szubin: the Romet Bike Plant and the State Engineering Center. Andrzej Wichłacz was entrusted with the duties of Chairman of the MKZ in Szubina. During March events in Bydgoszcz in 1981, as a representative of the MKZ, he took part in talks and decisions taken by the Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union of the Bydgoszcz region and at the NSZZ National Commission convention at the former railway rolling stock repair plant. In his martial law he did not give up his activities and collaborated with Fr. Jan Kątny and other oppositionists founded the pastoral ministry of people of work, whose purpose was to provide material assistance to families of interned activists of the Solidarity. In free Poland he continued his activities in the NSZZ.Solidarity, he was the chairman of the audit committee by four terms and a delegate to the general meeting of delegates at the National Heat Section commission. For his faithfulness to the ideals of Solidarity, he received in 2017 a medal of Solidarity: Deserved for the Bydgoszcz Region. He was a very modest man, after the ceremony at the Museum of the Szubińska Land, he said: This distinction is not for me, I think that all those who have passed the dark night of martial law deserved it. Andrzej Wwylach took an active part [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the cultural, social and political life of the municipality of Shubin. For many years he played and sang in music bands Karat and Apache. Always smiling, happy, sharing his talent with band members and audiences. He was Council of Seniors of the Municipality of Szubin in the term of office of 2015.2019, co-founder and long-term president of the Association of the Joint Municipality of Szubin. For many years he presided over the organization of Flag Day, thanks to him the celebration of 2 May in Szubina grew to the county rank and received support from the Marshal of Kujawsko-Pomorskie Voivodeship. He organized trips for members and sympathizers of the Association of Free Municipality of Szubin to Warsaw, Gdansk, Wrocław, planned the next: to Olsztyn, Vilnius. Mortal and long-term illness has interrupted life and social activity. For friends and friends he was a solid, honest partner and reliable support. We're gonna miss him so much. With gratitude for your friend and compassion for your family. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2020-11-27-sejm-21-3.u584	Thank you very much, Member. Mr Waldemar Andzel, the Law and Justice Club. Two minutes, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2020-11-27-sejm-21-3.u585	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 19 November 1885 was born a gene. Kazimierz Sosnkowski, independence activist, Chief of Staff of the First Brigade of the Polish Legions, Minister of Military Affairs, Chief Commandant of the Armed Fighting Union, Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of State in the Government of the Republic of Poland in exile, Chief Leader of the Polish Armed Forces. His military and political activity dates back to the time of Józef Piłsudski. A long-standing relationship, a joint struggle for independence, the creation of the Strzelecki Union and a joint stay in Magdeburg detention have turned into friendship between these two outstanding personalities of Polish history. However, during the May bombing, Sosnkowski was unable to take a stand on either side, resulting in a suicide attempt by shooting himself in the chest. The general's hospitalization and recovery lasted over a year. After the outbreak of the First World War, Sosnkowski became Chief of Staff of the First Polish Legions Brigade. At the end of 1918, with the rank of Brigadier General in the uprising Polish Army, he commanded the General District of Warsaw. He mobilized a Polish military organization. He also became Deputy Minister of Military Affairs. A year later, he was briefly at the head of the reserve army. During the Polish-Bolshevik War he became Minister of Military Affairs, Member of the Council of State Defence and Commander of the District of Corps No. VII in Poznań. Before the outbreak of World War II, he became an officer of the armed forces, and after the outbreak he commanded the South Front. The failed attempt to connect the Army with the Army of Kraków and consequently the inability to organize the defence front caused Sosnkowski to recommend troops to enter Hungary. Sam made it to Budapest, and from there with the help of the Hungarians to France. The President of the Republic of Poland Władysław Raczkiewicz appointed him his successor. As Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of State, he opposed the Sikorski-Majski Agreement. After Gen. Sikorski's death, he became chief leader of the Polish Armed Forces. In 1944, he was decommissioned and emigrated to Canada. Because of his anti-communist views, he was repeatedly denied a visa to the United Kingdom and America. It was thanks to Sosnkowski's emigration that the act of national unification was signed. Glory to his memory. Thank you very much.
